\Gamma\nvdash\Delta
and
(p_{1},p_{2})\,\sigma=(p_{1}\sigma,p_{2}\sigma)=(p_{1}\sigma_{1},p_{2}\sigma_{2})
, applying
Lemma

D.7
(
Inversion
) to
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}(p_{1},p_{2})\,\sigma:A,\,\Delta
, we obtain:

(12)

\exists A_{1},\,A_{2}\text{ such that }(A_{1},A_{2})\sqsubseteq A\;\land\;\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}p_{1}\sigma_{1}:A_{1},\,\Delta\;\land\;\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}p_{2}\sigma_{2}:A_{2},\,\Delta

Furthermore, since
(p_{1},p_{2})\,\sigma
is a value, both
p_{1}\sigma_{1}
and
p_{2}\sigma_{2}
must be values. By applying the induction hypothesis to the type judgments in
Equation

12
, there exist
\theta_{1}
with
\textbf{dom}(\theta_{1})=\mathsf{FV}(p_{1})
and
\theta_{2}
with
\textbf{dom}(\theta_{2})=\mathsf{FV}(p_{2})
, such that:

(IH.1)

\forall x\in\textbf{dom}(\theta_{1}).\,\theta_{1}(x)\sqsubseteq\mathsf{Ok}
and
\forall x\in\textbf{dom}(\theta_{2}).\,\theta_{2}(x)\sqsubseteq\mathsf{Ok}

(IH.2)

\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\sigma_{1}\mathrel{::}\theta_{1},\,\Delta
and
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\sigma_{2}\mathrel{::}\theta_{2},\,\Delta

(IH.3)

\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}p\sigma_{1}:p_{1}\theta,\,\Delta
where
p_{1}\theta_{1}\sqsubseteq\mathsf{Ok}\land p_{1}\theta_{1}\sqsubseteq A_{1}
and
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}p\sigma_{2}:p_{2}\theta,\,\Delta
where
p_{2}\theta_{2}\sqsubseteq\mathsf{Ok}\land p_{2}\theta_{2}\sqsubseteq A_{2}

Define
\theta
to be the substitution:

\theta(x)=\begin{cases}\theta_{1}(x)&\text{if }x\in\textbf{dom}(\theta_{1}),\\
\theta_{2}(x)&\text{if }x\in\textbf{dom}(\theta_{2}).\end{cases}

where
\textbf{dom}(\theta)=\textbf{dom}(\theta_{1})\cup\textbf{dom}(\theta_{2})
. Then
\textbf{dom}(\theta)=\textbf{dom}(\sigma)=\mathsf{FV}(p)
. Now we check that
\theta
satisfies:

(1)

\forall x\in\textbf{dom}(\theta).\,\theta(x)\sqsubseteq\mathsf{Ok}
:

By definition of
\theta
and (IH.1).

(2)

\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\sigma\mathrel{::}\theta,\,\Delta
:

By
Definition

D.5
, (IH.2) implies:

(13)

\forall x\in\textbf{dom}(\theta_{i}).\,\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\sigma_{i}(x):\theta_{i}(x)\quad i\in\{1,2\}

By definition of
\sigma_{1}
,
\sigma_{2}
and
\theta
, we have:

(14)

\forall x\in\textbf{dom}(\theta_{i}).\,\sigma_{i}(x)=\sigma(x)\text{ and }\theta(x)=\theta_{i}(x)\quad i\in\{1,2\}

Substituting
Equation

14
into
Equation

13
gives:

(15)

\forall x\in\textbf{dom}(\theta_{i}).\,\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\sigma(x):\theta(x)\quad i\in\{1,2\}

Moreover, as
\textbf{dom}(\theta)=\textbf{dom}(\theta_{1})\cup\textbf{dom}(\theta_{2})
, for any
x\in\textbf{dom}(\theta)
, either
x\in\textbf{dom}(\theta_{1})
or
x\in\textbf{dom}(\theta_{2})
. This combined with
Equation

15
implies:

\forall x\in\textbf{dom}(\theta).\,\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\sigma(x):\theta(x)

Since
\textbf{dom}(\theta)=\textbf{dom}(\sigma)
, by
Definition

D.5
, this shows
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\sigma\mathrel{::}\theta,\,\Delta
by
Definition

D.5
.

(3)

\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}(p_{1},p_{2})\,\sigma:(p_{1},p_{2})\,\theta
, where
(p_{1},p_{2})\,\theta\sqsubseteq\mathsf{Ok}\land(p_{1},p_{2})\,\theta\sqsubseteq A

Applying the typing rule
Pair
to the type judgements in (IH.3), we have:

(16)

\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}(p_{1}\sigma_{1},p_{2}\sigma_{2}):(p_{1}\theta_{1},p_{2}\theta_{2}),\,\Delta

By definition of
\sigma_{1}
,
\sigma_{2}
and
\theta
,
(p_{1},p_{2})\,\sigma=(p_{1}\sigma,p_{2}\sigma)=(p_{1}\sigma_{1},p_{2}\sigma_{2})
and
(p_{1},p_{2})\,\theta=(p_{1}\theta,p_{2}\theta)=(p_{1}\theta_{1},p_{2}\theta_{2})
. Substituting
(p_{1}\sigma_{1},p_{2}\sigma_{2})=(p_{1},p_{2})\,\sigma
and
(p_{1}\theta_{1},p_{2}\theta_{2})=(p_{1},p_{2})\,\theta
into
Equation

16
, we get:

\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}(p_{1},p_{2})\,\sigma:(p_{1},p_{2})\,\theta,\,\Delta

From (IH.3), we also have
p_{1}\theta_{1}\sqsubseteq\mathsf{Ok}\land p_{1}\theta_{1}\sqsubseteq A_{1}
and
p_{2}\theta_{2}\sqsubseteq\mathsf{Ok}\land p_{2}\theta_{2}\sqsubseteq A_{2}
. Applying the subtyping rule
Pair
with
p_{1}\theta_{1}\sqsubseteq\mathsf{Ok}
and
p_{2}\theta_{2}\sqsubseteq\mathsf{Ok}
gives
(p_{1}\theta_{1},p_{2}\theta_{2})\sqsubseteq\mathsf{Pair}
. Combining this with
\mathsf{Pair}\sqsubseteq\mathsf{Ok}
via transitivity, we have
(p_{1}\theta_{1},p_{2}\theta_{2})\sqsubseteq\mathsf{Ok}
. Similarly, applying the subtyping rule
Pair
with
p_{1}\theta_{1}\sqsubseteq A_{1}
and
p_{2}\theta_{2}\sqsubseteq A_{2}
yields
(p_{1}\theta_{1},p_{2}\theta_{2})\sqsubseteq(A_{1},A_{2})
. Since we also know
(A_{1},A_{2})\sqsubseteq A
from inversion, transitivity gives
(p_{1}\theta_{1},p_{2}\theta_{2})\sqsubseteq A
. Finally, Substituting
(p_{1}\sigma_{1},p_{2}\sigma_{2})=(p_{1},p_{2})\,\sigma
and
(p_{1}\theta_{1},p_{2}\theta_{2})=(p_{1},p_{2})\,\theta
into
(p_{1}\theta_{1},p_{2}\theta_{2})\sqsubseteq\mathsf{Ok}
and
(p_{1}\theta_{1},p_{2}\theta_{2})\sqsubseteq A
, we conclude that
(p_{1},p_{2})\,\theta\sqsubseteq\mathsf{Ok}\land(p_{1},p_{2})\,\theta\sqsubseteq A
.

∎

Lemma D.22 (Preservation for Redex Reduction).

Let
R
be a redex. If
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}R:A,\,\Delta
and
R\,\ \mathord{\rhd}\,\ R^{\prime}
, then
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}R^{\prime}:A,\,\Delta
.

Proof.

We start with a simple case analysis on whether
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\Delta
.

•

Suppose
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\Delta
. Then by
Lemma

D.1
(
Weakening
), it follows immediately that we also have
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}R^{\prime}:A,\,\Delta
.

•

Otherwise,
\Gamma\nvdash\Delta
. In this case, we proceed by case analysis on the structure of the redex
R
.

Case
(
Beta
).
Suppose
R\,\ \mathord{\rhd}\,\ R^{\prime}
has shape
(\lambda x.\,M)\,N\,\ \mathord{\rhd}\,\ M[N/x]
. Then
R=(\lambda x.\,M)N
,
R^{\prime}=M[N/x]
and
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}(\lambda x.\,M)N:A,\,\Delta
. As
\Gamma\nvdash\Delta
, by
Lemma

D.7
(
Inversion
) on
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}(\lambda x.\,M)N:A,\,\Delta
, either:

1

\exists B,\,C\text{ such that }C\sqsubseteq A
and
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}N:B,\,\Delta
and
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\lambda x.\,M:B\to C,\,\Delta
;

2

or,
{\mathsf{Ok}}^{c}\sqsubseteq A
and
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\lambda x.\,M:{\mathsf{Fun}}^{c},\,\Delta
.

Assume
2
holds. Since
\Gamma\nvdash\Delta
, applying
Lemma

D.7
(
Inversion
) to
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\lambda x.\,M:{\mathsf{Fun}}^{c},\,\Delta
yields:

(17)

\exists B^{\prime},\,C^{\prime}\text{ such that }B^{\prime}\to C^{\prime}\sqsubseteq{\mathsf{Fun}}^{c}\,\land\,\Gamma,\,x:B^{\prime}\mathrel{\vdash}M:C^{\prime},\,\Delta

Moreover, by the subtyping rule
Fun
, we have
B^{\prime}\to C^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\mathsf{Fun}
(recall that
\mathsf{Fun}=\mathsf{Bot}\to\mathsf{Top}
by definition). Therefore, we have both
B^{\prime}\to C^{\prime}\sqsubseteq{\mathsf{Fun}}^{c}
(from
Equation

17
) and
B^{\prime}\to C^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\mathsf{Fun}
. By
Theorem

6.1
(
Soundness of Subtyping
), this implies
[\![B^{\prime}\to C^{\prime}]\!]\subseteq[\![{\mathsf{Fun}}^{c}]\!]
and
[\![B^{\prime}\to C^{\prime}]\!]\subseteq[\![\mathsf{Fun}]\!]
. Consequently,

(18)

[\![B^{\prime}\to C^{\prime}]\!]\subseteq[\![\mathsf{Fun}]\!]\cap[\![{\mathsf{Fun}}^{c}]\!]

By
Definition

3.1
,
[\![\mathsf{Fun}]\!]\cap[\![{\mathsf{Fun}}^{c}]\!]=[\![\mathsf{Fun}]\!]\cap[\![\mathsf{Top}]\!]\setminus[\![\mathsf{Fun}]\!]=\varnothing
. Substituting this into
Equation

18
gives
[\![B^{\prime}\to C^{\prime}]\!]\subseteq\varnothing
, and thus
[\![B^{\prime}\to C^{\prime}]\!]=\varnothing
.
However,
Lemma

D.8
states that
\lambda x.\,\textsf{\lx@text@underline{\smash{div}}}\in[\![B^{\prime}\to C^{\prime}]\!]
, which contradicts
[\![B^{\prime}\to C^{\prime}]\!]=\varnothing
. Therefore, our assumption that
2
holds fails, leaving case
1
as the only possibility.

From
1
,
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\lambda x.\,M:B\to C,\,\Delta
. Since
\Gamma\nvdash\Delta
, applying
Lemma

D.7
(
Inversion
), we have:

(19)

\exists B^{\prime},\,C^{\prime}\text{ such that }B^{\prime}\to C^{\prime}\sqsubseteq B\to C\,\land\,\Gamma,\,x:B^{\prime}\mathrel{\vdash}M:C^{\prime},\,\Delta

By
Lemma

D.17
,
B^{\prime}\to C^{\prime}\sqsubseteq B\to C
implies
B\sqsubseteq B^{\prime}
and
C^{\prime}\sqsubseteq C
. Thus we can apply
Lemma

D.2
to
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}N:B,\,\Delta
(from
1
) and
\Gamma,\,x:B^{\prime}\mathrel{\vdash}M:C^{\prime},\,\Delta
(from
Equation

19
) to obtain
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}M[{N}/{x}]:C^{\prime},\,\Delta
. Moreover, combining
C^{\prime}\sqsubseteq C
and
C\sqsubseteq A
via transitivity gives
C^{\prime}\sqsubseteq A
. By the typing rule
Sub
, this conbined with
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}M[{N}/{x}]:C^{\prime},\,\Delta
implies
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}M[{N}/{x}]:A,\,\Delta
. Given that
M[{N}/{x}]=R^{\prime}
, this completes the proof of the case.

Case
(
Fix
).
Suppose
R\,\ \mathord{\rhd}\,\ R^{\prime}
has shape
\mathsf{fix}\,x.\,M\,\ \mathord{\rhd}\,\ M[\mathsf{fix}\,x.\,M/x]
.
Then
R=\mathsf{fix}\,x.\,M
,
R^{\prime}=M[\mathsf{fix}\,x.\,M/x]
and
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\mathsf{fix}\,x.\,M:A,\,\Delta
. Since
\Gamma\nvdash\Delta
,
by
Lemma

D.7
(
Inversion
):

(20)

\exists A^{\prime}\text{ such that }A^{\prime}\sqsubseteq A\text{ and }\Gamma,\,x:A^{\prime}\mathrel{\vdash}M:A^{\prime},\,\Delta

Applying the typing rule
Fix
back to
Equation

20
, we derive:

(21)

\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\mathsf{fix}\,x.\,M:A^{\prime},\,\Delta

By
Lemma

D.2
(
Substitution
),
Equation

20
and
Equation

21
implies
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}M[{\mathsf{fix}\,x.\,M}/{x}]:A^{\prime},\,\Delta
. Since
A^{\prime}\sqsubseteq A
, applying the typing rule
Sub
gives
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}M[{\mathsf{fix}\,x.\,M}/{x}]:A,\,\Delta
. Given that
R^{\prime}=M[{\mathsf{fix}\,x.\,M}/{x}]:A^{\prime}
, this proves
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}R^{\prime}:A,\,\Delta
.

Case
(
Op
).
Suppose
R\,\ \mathord{\rhd}\,\ R^{\prime}
has shape
\underline{n}\otimes\underline{m}\,\ \mathord{\rhd}\,\ \underline{n\otimes m}
.
Then
R=\underline{n}\otimes\underline{m}
,
R^{\prime}=\underline{n\otimes m}
and
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\underline{n}\otimes\underline{m}:A,\,\Delta
. Since
\Gamma\nvdash\Delta
, by
Lemma

D.7
(
Inversion
) on
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\underline{n}\otimes\underline{m}:A,\,\Delta
, either:

1

\mathsf{Int}\sqsubseteq A
and
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\underline{n}:\mathsf{Int},\,\Delta
and
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\underline{m}:\mathsf{Int},\,\Delta

2

or,
{\mathsf{Ok}}^{c}\sqsubseteq A
and
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\underline{m}:{\mathsf{Int}}^{c},\,\underline{n}:{\mathsf{Int}}^{c},\,\Delta
.

Assume
2
holds. Since
\Gamma\nvdash\Delta
, applying
Lemma

D.7
(
Inversion
) to
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\underline{m}:{\mathsf{Int}}^{c},\,\underline{n}:{\mathsf{Int}}^{c},\,\Delta
yields:

\displaystyle\text{Either }\mathsf{Int}\sqsubseteq{\mathsf{Int}}^{c}\text{ or }\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\underline{n}:{\mathsf{Int}}^{c},\,\Delta

By
Theorem

6.1
(
Soundness of Subtyping
),
\mathsf{Int}\sqsubseteq{\mathsf{Int}}^{c}
implies
[\![\mathsf{Int}]\!]\subseteq[\![{\mathsf{Int}}^{c}]\!]
. But by
Definition

3.1
, this is impossible. Consequently,
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\underline{n}:{\mathsf{Int}}^{c},\,\Delta
must hold. However, since we also have
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\underline{n}:\mathsf{Int},\,\Delta
(by the typing rule
Int
), it follows from
Lemma

D.10
that
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\Delta
. Since we are in the case where
\Gamma\nvdash\Delta
,
2
has leaded to contradiction, leaving
1
as the only possibility.

From
1
,
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\underline{n}:\mathsf{Int},\,\Delta
and
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\underline{m}:\mathsf{Int},\,\Delta
. Applying the Op rule then yields
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\underline{m}\otimes\underline{n}:\mathsf{Int},\,\Delta
. Since
R^{\prime}=\underline{m}\otimes\underline{n}
, this proves
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}R^{\prime}:A,\,\Delta
.

Case
(
Match-Hit
).
Suppose
R\,\ \mathord{\rhd}\,\ R^{\prime}
has shape:

\mathsf{match}\,V\,\mathsf{with}\,\{\mid_{i=1}^{k}p_{i}\mapsto N_{i}\}\,\ \mathord{\rhd}\,\ N_{j}\sigma\quad(V=N_{j}\sigma)

Since
V=p_{j}\sigma
for some
1\leq j\leq k
,
R=\mathsf{match}\,p_{j}\sigma\,\mathsf{with}\,\{\mid_{i=1}^{k}p_{i}\mapsto N_{i}\}
and
R^{\prime}=N_{j}\sigma
. Moreover:

(22)

\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\mathsf{match}\,p_{j}\sigma\,\mathsf{with}\,\{\mid_{i=1}^{k}p_{i}\mapsto N_{i}\}:A,\,\Delta

Applying
Lemma

D.7
(
Inversion
) to
Equation

22
with
\Gamma\nvdash\Delta
, we obtain that one of the following holds:

1

\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}p_{j}\sigma:\textstyle\bigvee_{i=1}^{k}p_{i}\theta_{i},\,\Delta
and
\forall i\in[1,k].\,\Gamma,\,\theta_{i}\mathrel{\vdash}p_{j}\sigma:{(p_{i}\theta_{i})}^{c},\,N_{i}:A,\,\Delta

2

or,
{\mathsf{Ok}}^{c}\sqsubseteq A
and
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}p_{j}\sigma:{(\textstyle\bigvee_{i=1}^{k}p_{i}{\color[rgb]{0.3984,0.1992,0.3984}{[}}\mathsf{Ok}/x|x\in\mathsf{FV}(p_{i})])}^{c},\,\Delta
.

Since
\Gamma\nvdash\Delta
and
p_{j}\sigma=V
is a value, by
Lemma

D.21
, for any type
C
where
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}p_{j}\sigma:C,\,\Delta
, there exists a type substitution
\theta
with
\textbf{dom}(\theta)=\mathsf{FV}(p_{j})
satisfying:

(1)

\forall x\in\textbf{dom}(\theta).\theta(x)\sqsubseteq\mathsf{Ok}
, and

(2)

\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\sigma\mathrel{::}\theta,\,\Delta

(3)

\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}p_{j}\sigma:p_{j}\theta,\,\Delta
where
p_{j}\theta\sqsubseteq\mathsf{Ok}\land p_{j}\theta\sqsubseteq C

Assume
2
holds, then:

(23)

\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}p_{j}\sigma:{(\textstyle\bigvee_{i=1}^{k}p_{i}{\color[rgb]{0.3984,0.1992,0.3984}{[}}\mathsf{Ok}/x|x\in\mathsf{FV}(p_{i})])}^{c},\,\Delta

Thus there exists
\theta
satistifying (1)–(3) for
C={(\textstyle\bigvee_{i=1}^{k}p_{i}{\color[rgb]{0.3984,0.1992,0.3984}{[}}\mathsf{Ok}/x|x\in\mathsf{FV}(p_{i})])}^{c}
.
It is immediate from condition (1) that
\theta\sqsubseteq{\color[rgb]{0.3984,0.1992,0.3984}{[}}\mathsf{Ok}/x|x\in\mathsf{FV}(p_{j})]
. Thus by the
\Leftarrow
directon of
Lemma

D.4
,
p_{j}\theta\sqsubseteq p_{j}{\color[rgb]{0.3984,0.1992,0.3984}{[}}\mathsf{Ok}/x|x\in\mathsf{FV}(p_{j})]
. Since
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}p_{j}\sigma:p_{j}\theta,\,\Delta
(from condition (3)), applying the typing rule
Sub
, we derive:

(24)

\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}p_{j}\sigma:p_{j}{\color[rgb]{0.3984,0.1992,0.3984}{[}}\mathsf{Ok}/x|x\in\mathsf{FV}(p_{j})],\,\Delta

By
(UnionR)
and the order-reversing property of complement (
Lemma

A.1
),
{(\textstyle\bigvee_{i=1}^{k}p_{i}{\color[rgb]{0.3984,0.1992,0.3984}{[}}\mathsf{Ok}/x|x\in\mathsf{FV}(p_{i})])}^{c}\sqsubseteq{(p_{j}{\color[rgb]{0.3984,0.1992,0.3984}{[}}\mathsf{Ok}/x|x\in\mathsf{FV}(p_{j})])}^{c}
. Therefore,
Lemma

D.10
,
Equation

23
and
Equation

24
implies
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\Delta
. As we are in the case where
\Gamma\nvdash\Delta
, our assumption that
2
holds leads to a contradiction. Thus
1
must hold.

From
1
,
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}p_{j}\sigma:\textstyle\bigvee_{i=1}^{k}p_{i}\theta_{i},\,\Delta
(where
p_{j}\sigma=V
).
Thus there exists
\theta
satistifying (1)–(3) for
C=\textstyle\bigvee_{i=1}^{k}p_{i}\theta_{i}
. In this case, condition (3) says:

\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}p_{j}\sigma:p_{j}\theta,\,\Delta\quad\text{ where }p_{j}\theta\sqsubseteq\mathsf{Ok}\land p_{j}\theta\sqsubseteq\textstyle\bigvee_{i=1}^{k}p_{i}\theta_{i}

Applying
Lemma

D.17
to
p_{j}\theta\sqsubseteq\textstyle\bigvee_{i=1}^{k}p_{i}\theta_{i}
. Then:

(1)

If
p_{j}\theta
is a complement or a union type, it has to be
k=1
, where
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}p_{j}\sigma:p_{j}\theta,\,\Delta
and
j=1
.

(2)

Otherwise,
p_{j}\theta
is neither a complement nor a union. Thus we can apply
Lemma

D.17
to obtain
\exists i\in[1,k].\,p_{j}\theta\sqsubseteq p_{i}\theta_{i}
. Given that patterns are disjoint, it has to be
i=j
and
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}p_{j}\sigma:p_{j}\theta,\,\Delta

In both cases,
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}p_{j}\sigma:p_{j}\theta,\,\Delta
. Then by the
\Rightarrow
direction of
Lemma

D.4
,
\theta\sqsubseteq\theta_{j}
. We also have
\forall i\in[1,k].\,\Gamma,\,\theta_{i}\mathrel{\vdash}p_{j}\sigma:{(p_{i}\theta_{i})}^{c},\,N_{i}:A,\,\Delta
from
1
. In particular, when
i=j
:

(25)

\Gamma,\,\theta_{j}\mathrel{\vdash}N_{j}:A,\,p_{j}\sigma:{(p_{j}\theta_{j})}^{c},\,\Delta

Having established
Equation

25
,
\theta\sqsubseteq\theta_{j}
and
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\sigma\mathrel{::}\theta,\,\Delta
(from condition (2)), we now verify
\textbf{dom}(\theta)\cap\mathsf{FV}(\{p_{j}\sigma:{(p_{j}\theta_{j})}^{c}\}\cup\Delta)=\varnothing
to be able to apply
Lemma

D.6
(
Simultaneous Substitution
). Given that
\mathsf{FV}(p_{1},\ldots,p_{k})\cap\mathsf{FV}(\Gamma\cup\Delta)=\varnothing
,
\mathsf{FV}(p_{i})\cap\mathsf{FV}(\Delta)=\varnothing
holds for all
i\in[1,k]
. In particular, setting
i=j
gives
\mathsf{FV}(p_{j})\cap\mathsf{FV}(\Delta)=\varnothing
. Since
\textbf{dom}(\theta)=\mathsf{FV}(p_{j})
, it follows that
\textbf{dom}(\theta)\cap\mathsf{FV}(\Delta)=\varnothing
. Furthermore, as
p_{j}\sigma
is a closed value,
\mathsf{FV}(p_{j}\sigma)=\varnothing
. Thus
\textbf{dom}(\theta)\cap\mathsf{FV}(\{p_{j}\sigma:{(p_{j}\theta_{j})}^{c}\}\cup\Delta)=(\textbf{dom}(\theta)\cap\mathsf{FV}(p_{j}\sigma))\cup(\textbf{dom}(\theta)\cap\mathsf{FV}(\Delta)=\varnothing)
.

Now, applying
Lemma

D.6
, we have:

(26)

\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}N_{j}\sigma:A,\,p_{j}\sigma:{(p_{j}\theta_{j})}^{c},\,\Delta

Meanwhile, since
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}p_{j}\sigma:p_{j}\theta,\,\Delta
(from condition (3)), by applying
Lemma

D.1
(
Weakening
), we have:

(27)

\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}N_{j}\sigma:A,\,p_{j}\sigma:p_{j}\theta_{j},\,\Delta

By
Lemma

D.10
, since
{(p_{j}\theta_{j})}^{c}\sqsubseteq{(p_{j}\theta_{j})}^{c}
,
Equation

26
and
Equation

27
implies
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}N_{j}\sigma:A,\,\Delta
. Given that
R^{\prime}=N_{j}\sigma:A
, this proves
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}R^{\prime}:A,\,\Delta
.

Therefore, whenever
R
is a redex, we have shown that
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}R:A,\,\Delta
implies
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}R^{\prime}:A,\,\Delta
for
R\,\ \mathord{\rhd}\,\ R^{\prime}
.

∎

Theorem D.23 (Preservation).

Suppose
Q\,\ \mathord{\rhd}\,\ Q^{\prime}
. If
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}Q:A,\,\Delta
, then
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}Q^{\prime}:A,\,\Delta
.

Proof.

We start with a simple case analysis on whether
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\Delta
.

•

Suppose
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\Delta
. Then by
Lemma

D.1
(
Weakening
), it follows immediately that we also have
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}Q^{\prime}:A,\,\Delta
.

•

Otherwise,
\Gamma\nvdash\Delta
. By
Definition

2.2
, for any
Q\,\ \mathord{\rhd}\,\ Q^{\prime}
, there exists an evaluation context
\mathcal{E}
and a redex
R
, such that
Q=\mathcal{E}\mathcal{[}R]
and
Q^{\prime}=\mathcal{E}\mathcal{[}R^{\prime}]
for
R\,\ \mathord{\rhd}\,\ R^{\prime}
. Therefore, it suffices to show:

\forall R\,(R\text{ is a redex}\land R\,\ \mathord{\rhd}\,\ R^{\prime})\,\forall\mathcal{E}\,(\mathcal{E}\text{ is a context}).\;\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\mathcal{E}\mathcal{[}R]:A,\,\Delta\Rightarrow\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\mathcal{E}\mathcal{[}R^{\prime}]:A,\,\Delta

We proceed by induction on the structure of
\mathcal{E}
.

Case
(
\mathcal{E}
has shape
[\cdot]
).
Here, given
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}R:A,\,\Delta
, we must show
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}R^{\prime}:A,\,\Delta
. Since
R
is a redex where
R\,\ \mathord{\rhd}\,\ R^{\prime}
, this follows directly from
Lemma

D.22
.

Case
(
\mathcal{E}
has shape
\mathcal{F}\,N
).
Here, given
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}(\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R]){N}:A,\,\Delta
, we must show
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}(\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R^{\prime}]){N}:A,\,\Delta
. By
Lemma

D.7
(
Inversion
) on
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}(\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R]){N}:A,\,\Delta
with
\Gamma\nvdash\Delta
, either:

1

\exists B,C
with
C\sqsubseteq A
such that
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R]:B\to C,\,\Delta
and
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}N:B,\,\Delta

2

{\mathsf{Ok}}^{c}\sqsubseteq A
and
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R]:{\mathsf{Fun}}^{c},\,\Delta
.

Case
1
.

Since
R
is a redex with
R\,\ \mathord{\rhd}\,\ R^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{F}
is a context, the induction hypothesis applied to
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R]:B\to C,\,\Delta
gives
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R^{\prime}]:B\to C,\,\Delta
. Applying the typing rule
App
to
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R^{\prime}]:B\to C,\,\Delta
and
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}N:B,\,\Delta
gives
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}(\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R^{\prime}]){N}:C,\,\Delta
. Since
C\sqsubseteq A
, the typing rule
Sub
applied to
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}(\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R^{\prime}]){N}:C,\,\Delta
gives
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}(\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R^{\prime}]){N}:A,\,\Delta
.

Case
2
.

By the induction hypothesis,
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R]:{\mathsf{Fun}}^{c},\,\Delta
implies
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R^{\prime}]:{\mathsf{Fun}}^{c},\,\Delta
. Applying the typing rule
AppE
to
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R^{\prime}]:{\mathsf{Fun}}^{c},\,\Delta
then yields
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}(\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R^{\prime}]){N}:{\mathsf{Ok}}^{c},\,\Delta
. Since
{\mathsf{Ok}}^{c}\sqsubseteq A
, the typing rule
Sub
applied to
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}(\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R^{\prime}]){N}:{\mathsf{Ok}}^{c},\,\Delta
gives
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}(\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R^{\prime}]){N}:A,\,\Delta
.

Case
(
\mathcal{E}
has shape
\pi_{i}(\mathcal{F})\,(i\in\{1,2\})
).
Here, given
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\pi_{i}(\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R]):A,\,\Delta
, we must show
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\pi_{i}(\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R^{\prime}]):A,\,\Delta
. By
Lemma

D.7
(
Inversion
) on
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\pi_{i}(\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R]):A,\,\Delta
with
\Gamma\nvdash\Delta
, either:

1

\exists A_{1},\,A_{2}
such that
A_{i}\sqsubseteq A
and
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R]:(A_{1},A_{2}),\,\Delta

2

{\mathsf{Ok}}^{c}\sqsubseteq A
and
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R^{\prime}]:{\mathsf{Pair}}^{c},\,\Delta
.

Case
1
.

Since
R
is a redex with
R\,\ \mathord{\rhd}\,\ R^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{F}
is a context, the induction hypothesis applied to
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R]:(A_{1},A_{2}),\,\Delta
gives
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R^{\prime}]:(A_{1},A_{2}),\,\Delta
. Applying the typing rule
\textsc{Prj}_{i}
to
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}R^{\prime}:(A_{1},A_{2}),\,\Delta
then yields
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\pi_{i}(\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R^{\prime}]):A_{i},\,\Delta
. Since
A_{i}\sqsubseteq A
, the typing rule
Sub
applied to
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\pi_{i}(\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R^{\prime}]):A_{i},\,\Delta
gives
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\pi_{i}(\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R^{\prime}]):A,\,\Delta
.

Case
2
.

By the induction hypothesis,
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R]:{\mathsf{Pair}}^{c},\,\Delta
and
R\,\ \mathord{\rhd}\,\ R^{\prime}
implies
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R^{\prime}]:{\mathsf{Pair}}^{c},\,\Delta
. Then, by applying the typing rule
\textsc{PrjE}_{i}
to
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R^{\prime}]:{\mathsf{Pair}}^{c},\,\Delta
, we get
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\pi_{i}(\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R^{\prime}]):{\mathsf{Ok}}^{c},\,\Delta
. Since
{\mathsf{Ok}}^{c}\sqsubseteq A
, the typing rule
Sub
applied to
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\pi_{i}(\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R^{\prime}]):{\mathsf{Ok}}^{c},\,\Delta
gives
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\pi_{i}(\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R^{\prime}]):A,\,\Delta
.

Case
(
\mathcal{E}
has shape
(\mathcal{F},N)
).
Here, given
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}(\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R],N):A,\,\Delta
, we must show
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}(\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R^{\prime}],N):A,\,\Delta
. By
Lemma

D.7
(
Inversion
) on
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}(\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R],N):A,\,\Delta
with
\Gamma\nvdash\Delta
:

\exists B,C\text{ such that }(B,\,C)\sqsubseteq A\text{ and }\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R]:B,\,\Delta\text{ and }\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}N:C,\,\Delta

Since
R
is a redex with
R\,\ \mathord{\rhd}\,\ R^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{F}
is a context, the induction hypothesis applied to
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R]:B,\,\Delta
gives
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R^{\prime}]:B,\,\Delta
. Applying the typing rule
Pair
to
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R^{\prime}]:B,\,\Delta
and
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}N:C,\,\Delta
then yields
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}(\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R^{\prime}],N):(B,C),\,\Delta
. Since
(B,C)\sqsubseteq A
, the typing rule
Sub
applied to
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}(\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R^{\prime}],N):(B,C),\,\Delta
gives
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}(\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R^{\prime}],N):A,\,\Delta
.

Case
(
\mathcal{E}
has shape
\mathcal{F}\otimes N
).
Here, given
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R]\otimes N:A,\,\Delta
, we must show
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R^{\prime}]\otimes N:A,\,\Delta
. By
Lemma

D.7
(
Inversion
) on
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R]\otimes N:A,\,\Delta
with
\Gamma\nvdash\Delta
, either:

1

\mathsf{Int}\sqsubseteq A
and
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R]:\mathsf{Int},\,\Delta
and
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}N:\mathsf{Int},\,\Delta

2

{\mathsf{Ok}}^{c}\sqsubseteq A
and
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R]:{\mathsf{Int}}^{c},\,N:{\mathsf{Int}}^{c},\,\Delta
.

Case
1
.

Since
R
is a redex with
R\,\ \mathord{\rhd}\,\ R^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{F}
is a context, the induction hypothesis applied to
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R]:\mathsf{Int},\,\Delta
gives
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R^{\prime}]:\mathsf{Int},\,\Delta
. Applying the typing rule
Op
to
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R^{\prime}]:\mathsf{Int},\,\Delta
and
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}N:\mathsf{Int},\,\Delta
gives
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R^{\prime}]\otimes N:\mathsf{Int},\,\Delta
. Since
C\sqsubseteq A
, the typing rule
Sub
applied to
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R^{\prime}]\otimes N:\mathsf{Int},\,\Delta
gives
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R^{\prime}]\otimes N:A,\,\Delta
.

Case
2
.

By the induction hypothesis,
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R]:{\mathsf{Int}}^{c},\,N:{\mathsf{Int}}^{c},\,\Delta
implies
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R^{\prime}]:{\mathsf{Int}}^{c},\,N:{\mathsf{Int}}^{c},\,\Delta
. Applying the typing rule
OpE
then yields
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R^{\prime}]\otimes N:{\mathsf{Ok}}^{c},\,\Delta
. Since
{\mathsf{Ok}}^{c}\sqsubseteq A
, the typing rule
Sub
applied to
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R^{\prime}]\otimes N:{\mathsf{Ok}}^{c},\,\Delta
gives
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R^{\prime}]\otimes N:A,\,\Delta
.

Case
(
\mathcal{E}
has shape
V\otimes\mathcal{F}
).
Here, given
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}V\otimes\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R]:A,\,\Delta
, we must show
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}V\otimes\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R^{\prime}]:A,\,\Delta
. By
Lemma

D.7
(
Inversion
) on
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}V\otimes\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R]:A,\,\Delta
with
\Gamma\nvdash\Delta
, either:

1

\mathsf{Int}\sqsubseteq A
and
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}V:\mathsf{Int},\,\Delta
and
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R]:\mathsf{Int},\,\Delta

2

{\mathsf{Ok}}^{c}\sqsubseteq A
and
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}V:{\mathsf{Int}}^{c},\,\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R]:{\mathsf{Int}}^{c},\,\Delta
.

Case
1
.

Since
R
is a redex with
R\,\ \mathord{\rhd}\,\ R^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{F}
is a context, the induction hypothesis applied to
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R]:\mathsf{Int},\,\Delta
gives
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R^{\prime}]:\mathsf{Int},\,\Delta
. Applying the typing rule
Op
to
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}V:\mathsf{Int},\,\Delta
and
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R^{\prime}]:\mathsf{Int},\,\Delta
yields
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}V\otimes\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R^{\prime}]:\mathsf{Int},\,\Delta
. Since
C\sqsubseteq A
, the typing rule
Sub
applied to
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}V\otimes\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R^{\prime}]:\mathsf{Int},\,\Delta
gives
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}V\otimes\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R^{\prime}]:A,\,\Delta
.

Case
2
.

By the induction hypothesis,
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}V:{\mathsf{Int}}^{c},\,\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R]:{\mathsf{Int}}^{c},\,\Delta
implies
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}V:{\mathsf{Int}}^{c},\,\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R^{\prime}]:{\mathsf{Int}}^{c},\,\Delta
. Applying the typing rule
OpE
then yields
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}V\otimes\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R^{\prime}]:{\mathsf{Ok}}^{c},\,\Delta
. Since
{\mathsf{Ok}}^{c}\sqsubseteq A
, the typing rule
Sub
applied to
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}V\otimes\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R^{\prime}]:{\mathsf{Ok}}^{c},\,\Delta
gives
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}V\otimes\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R^{\prime}]:A,\,\Delta
.

Case
(
\mathcal{E}
has shape
\mathsf{match}\,\mathcal{F}\,\mathsf{with}\,\{\mid_{i=1}^{k}p_{i}\mapsto N_{i}\}
).
Here, given
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\mathsf{match}\,\mathcal{F}\,\mathsf{with}\,\{\mid_{i=1}^{k}p_{i}\mapsto N_{i}\}:A,\,\Delta
, we must show
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\mathsf{match}\,\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R^{\prime}]\,\mathsf{with}\,\{\mid_{i=1}^{k}p_{i}\mapsto N_{i}\}:A,\,\Delta
. By
Lemma

D.7
(
Inversion
) on
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\mathsf{match}\,\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R]\,\mathsf{with}\,\{\mid_{i=1}^{k}p_{i}\mapsto N_{i}\}:A,\,\Delta
with
\Gamma\nvdash\Delta
, either:

1

\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R]:\textstyle\bigvee_{i=1}^{k}p_{i}\theta_{i},\,\Delta
and
\forall i\in[1,k].\,\Gamma,\,\theta_{i}\mathrel{\vdash}\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R]:{(p_{i}\theta_{i})}^{c},\,N_{i}:A,\,\Delta

2

{\mathsf{Ok}}^{c}\sqsubseteq A
and
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R]:{(\textstyle\bigvee_{i=1}^{k}p_{i}{\color[rgb]{0.3984,0.1992,0.3984}{[}}\mathsf{Ok}/x|x\in\mathsf{FV}(p_{i})])}^{c},\,\Delta
.

Case
1
.

Since
R
is a redex with
R\,\ \mathord{\rhd}\,\ R^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{F}
is a context, the induction hypothesis applied to
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R]:\textstyle\bigvee_{i=1}^{k}p_{i}\theta_{i},\,\Delta
gives
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R^{\prime}]:\textstyle\bigvee_{i=1}^{k}p_{i}\theta_{i},\,\Delta
. Applying the typing rule
Match
to
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R^{\prime}]:\textstyle\bigvee_{i=1}^{k}p_{i}\theta_{i},\,\Delta
and
\forall i\in[1,k].\,\Gamma,\,\theta_{i}\mathrel{\vdash}\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R^{\prime}]:{(p_{i}\theta_{i})}^{c},\,N_{i}:A,\,\Delta
, we get
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\mathsf{match}\,\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R^{\prime}]\,\mathsf{with}\,\{\mid_{i=1}^{k}p_{i}\mapsto N_{i}\}:A,\,\Delta
.

Case
2
.

By the induction hypothesis,
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R]:{(\textstyle\bigvee_{i=1}^{k}p_{i}{\color[rgb]{0.3984,0.1992,0.3984}{[}}\mathsf{Ok}/x|x\in\mathsf{FV}(p_{i})])}^{c},\,\Delta
implies
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R^{\prime}]:{(\textstyle\bigvee_{i=1}^{k}p_{i}{\color[rgb]{0.3984,0.1992,0.3984}{[}}\mathsf{Ok}/x|x\in\mathsf{FV}(p_{i})])}^{c},\,\Delta
. Applying the typing rule
MatchE
then yields
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\mathsf{match}\,\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R^{\prime}]\,\mathsf{with}\,\{\mid_{i=1}^{k}p_{i}\mapsto N_{i}\}:{\mathsf{Ok}}^{c},\,\Delta
. Since
{\mathsf{Ok}}^{c}\sqsubseteq A
, the typing rule
Sub
applied to
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\mathsf{match}\,\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R^{\prime}]\,\mathsf{with}\,\{\mid_{i=1}^{k}p_{i}\mapsto N_{i}\}:{\mathsf{Ok}}^{c},\,\Delta
gives
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}\mathsf{match}\,\mathcal{F}\mathcal{[}R^{\prime}]\,\mathsf{with}\,\{\mid_{i=1}^{k}p_{i}\mapsto N_{i}\}:A,\,\Delta
.

∎

D.2.
Progress

Theorem D.24 (Progress).

Let
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}Q:A,\,\Delta
, where
\Gamma\nvdash\Delta
and
Q
is a closed term.

(1)

If
A\sqsubseteq\mathsf{Ok}
, then either
Q
can reduce one step, or
Q
is a value.

(2)

If
A\sqsubseteq{\mathsf{Ok}}^{c}
, then either
Q
can reduce one step, or
Q
is stuck.

Proof of
Theorem

D.24
.(1).

If
Q
can make a step, then the result is clear. Otherwise
Q
is a normal form and is, therefore, either a value
V
or a stuck term
S
. For (i), suppose
A\sqsubseteq\mathsf{Ok}
. Then also
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}Q:\mathsf{Ok},\,\Delta
. If
Q
is a stuck term, then by Lemma
D.16
(and weakening),
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}Q:{\mathsf{Ok}}^{c},\,\Delta
, but then we obtain a contradiction by cut (Lemma
D.19
), so
Q
must be a value. For (ii), suppose
A\sqsubseteq{\mathsf{Ok}}^{c}
. Then also
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}Q:{\mathsf{Ok}}^{c},\,\Delta
. If
Q
is a value, then by Lemma
D.9
, it follows that
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}Q:\mathsf{Ok},\,\Delta
and, again, we obtain a contradiction by cut (Lemma
D.19
), so
Q
must be stuck.
∎

D.3.
Completeness

Theorem
7.5
:

For all normal forms
U
, both of the following are true:

(i) If
U
is a value then
\ \mathrel{\vdash}U:\mathsf{Ok}
.   (ii) If
U
is stuck then
\ \mathrel{\vdash}U:{\mathsf{Ok}}^{c}

Proof.

This follows immediately from Lemmas
D.9
and
D.16
.
∎
